--- LINES 417-447 ---
     417:  * (organization_id, engagement_id, requirement_set_id) identity, exactly the
     418:  * same derivation listEngagementRequirementSetsForOrganization uses for
     419:  * superseded_by_engagement_requirement_set_id, here applied directly to find
     420:  * "the current decision" for one governed identity rather than trusting a
     421:  * caller-supplied row id. This is what a governed replacement/lifecycle
     422:  * write (Package 2B-B) must look up before superseding: the current row may
     423:  * be the original non-authoritative proposal (Package 2B-A) or an already
     424:  * reviewed/confirmed decision (a Package 2B-B replacement). Scoped by
     425:  * organization_id and engagement_id so a caller can never resolve a row
     426:  * belonging to another tenant or engagement. Supports FOR UPDATE locking so
     427:  * a governed review transaction can serialize concurrent review attempts
     428:  * against the same identity; the partial unique index on
>>   429:  * supersedes_engagement_requirement_set_id (Package 2A) is still the actual
     430:  * conflict-safety backstop if two transactions race past this lock.
     431:  */
     432: export async function getCurrentEngagementRequirementSetForIdentity(
     433:   { organizationId, engagementId, requirementSetId, lockForUpdate = false },
     434:   db = pool,
     435: ) {

     440:             ers.engagement_id::text AS engagement_id,
     441:             ers.requirement_set_id::text AS requirement_set_id,

     445:        FROM kai.engagement_requirement_sets ers

     447:         AND ers.engagement_id = $2

     432: export async function getCurrentEngagementRequirementSetForIdentity(
     433:   { organizationId, engagementId, requirementSetId, lockForUpdate = false },
     434:   db = pool,
     435: ) {
     436:   if (!organizationId || !engagementId || !requirementSetId) return null;
     437:   const { rows } = await db.query(
     438:     `SELECT ers.engagement_requirement_set_id::text AS engagement_requirement_set_id,
     439:             ers.organization_id::text AS organization_id,
     440:             ers.engagement_id::text AS engagement_id,
     441:             ers.requirement_set_id::text AS requirement_set_id,
     442:             ers.applicability_status,
>>   443:             ers.applicability_effective_state,
>>   444:             ers.supersedes_engagement_requirement_set_id::text AS supersedes_engagement_requirement_set_id
     445:        FROM kai.engagement_requirement_sets ers
     446:       WHERE ers.organization_id = $1
     447:         AND ers.engagement_id = $2
     448:         AND ers.requirement_set_id = $3
     449:         AND NOT EXISTS (
     450:           SELECT 1
     451:             FROM kai.engagement_requirement_sets successor
>>   452:            WHERE successor.supersedes_engagement_requirement_set_id = ers.engagement_requirement_set_id
     453:         )
     454:       LIMIT 1${lockForUpdate ? " FOR UPDATE" : ""}`,
     455:     [organizationId, engagementId, requirementSetId],
     456:   );
     457:   return rows[0] || null;
     458: }

accepts only `organizationId`/`engagementId`/`requirementSetId`/`decision` (`decision` constrained to exactly `applicable`/`not_applicable`/`retired` - Package 2A's existing vocabulary, nothing invented); inside one governed transaction, re-reads the engagement `FOR UPDATE`, then resolves and locks "the current decision" for that identity via `getCurrentEngagementRequirementSetForIdentity` (never a client-supplied row id), re-validates the requirement authority is still governed/active, derives `target_context_identity` from the just-locked current engagement target (never from proposal/prior-review time or client input) and rejects if it no longer matches the requirement set's authority identity, then inserts the reviewed row superseding whatever was current, with reviewer identity/role/timestamp resolved entirely server-side, and a required same-transaction audit event. Inspected Package 2A's migration/rollback (append-only schema, insert-only replacement lineage, partial unique "one root row"/"one successor" indexes), the existing applicability reader (`listEngagementRequirementSetsForOrganization`) and classifier (`classifyEngagementFunderRequirementsState`, `isCurrentReviewedApplicability`) in `kaiEngagementContextService.js`, Package 1A's `updateEngagementRequirementTarget` governed-transaction skeleton (actor resolution -> `validateActorCanPerformOperation` -> `withTransaction` -> same-transaction `insertRequiredSuccessfulAuditEvent`), the KAI actor/authorization model (`kaiActorContext.js`, `kaiAuthorizationService.js`, `assistantBoundaryValidators.js`), the P2-10 coverage-review-decision precedent for a single fixed reviewer role, and the existing Package 1A/1B/2A test/verify conventions (`node --test`, dedicated runner-owned `DATABASE_URL_*` env vars, ephemeral local-PostgreSQL scripts). Confirmed the Package 2A schema needs no change to support replacement: the "current decision" for a governed identity is already exactly "the row with no successor," and the existing partial unique index on `supersedes_engagement_requirement_set_id` already permits an unbounded append-only chain, not just one review.
   19998: 
   19999: **Implementation:**
   20000: - `Backend/kai/db/kaiQueries.js` - four new query functions, no schema/migration: `getRequirementSetAuthority` (exact source_type/source_code/framework_code/framework_status identity for one requirement set); `insertEngagementRequirementSetProposal` (inserts the non-authoritative root row - `applicability_status = 'proposed'`, reviewed/target-snapshot fields left NULL, relying on Package 2A's existing "one root row per identity" partial unique index); `getCurrentEngagementRequirementSetForIdentity` (2B-B's generalization of 2B-A's original row-id lookup: resolves "the current decision" for one `(organization_id, engagement_id, requirement_set_id)` identity using the Package 2A current-authority predicate itself - a row with no successor - rather than trusting a caller-supplied row id; `FOR UPDATE`-capable); `insertEngagementRequirementSetReviewApproval` (inserts the reviewed row that supersedes whichever row was current - a proposal on a first review, or an already-reviewed decision on a replacement - relying on Package 2A's existing append-only trigger, reviewed-authority/effective-state/approved-target-shape checks, and "one successor per superseded row" partial unique index).
>> 20001: - `Backend/kai/services/kaiEngagementRequirementApplicabilityService.js` (new) - `proposeEngagementRequirementSetApplicability`: authorizes a mapped human actor (same allowed roles as `updateEngagementRequirementTarget`) or an automated ("system"/"assistant"/"ai") actor with no `actorUserId`; validates organization/tenant and engagement ownership; validates the requirement set exists, is external/governed (`source_type <> 'kai_standard'` and one of the governed external source types), and is on an active framework; validates the requirement set's own `source_code`/`framework_code` exactly matches the engagement's current governed target (Package 1A `project_metadata.engagement_requirement_target`) with no fuzzy/label inference; inserts the proposal and a required same-transaction audit event. `approveEngagementRequirementSetApplicability` (2B-B generalization): unconditionally requires a mapped human actor (an AI/system actor is rejected before any role check, exactly mirroring every other reviewed-decision service in this family) authorized under a single fixed `gk_reviewer` role (mirroring the P2-10 precedent); accepts only `organizationId`/`engagementId`/`requirementSetId`/`decision` (`decision` constrained to exactly `applicable`/`not_applicable`/`retired` - Package 2A's existing vocabulary, nothing invented); inside one governed transaction, re-reads the engagement `FOR UPDATE`, then resolves and locks "the current decision" for that identity via `getCurrentEngagementRequirementSetForIdentity` (never a client-supplied row id), re-validates the requirement authority is still governed/active, derives `target_context_identity` from the just-locked current engagement target (never from proposal/prior-review time or client input) and rejects if it no longer matches the requirement set's authority identity, then inserts the reviewed row superseding whatever was current, with reviewer identity/role/timestamp resolved entirely server-side, and a required same-transaction audit event. Neither function accepts `reviewed_by`, `reviewed_at`, `applicability_effective_state`, `target_context_identity`, or a supersession/row id from the caller - the input-shape validators reject any such extra field before authorization is even attempted.
   20002: - `Backend/kai/routes/sprint2IntakeApi.js` - `POST /admin/organizations/:organizationId/engagements/:engagementId/requirement-set-applicability-proposals` (body: `{ requirementSetId }` only, unchanged from 2B-A) and (2B-B reshaped) `POST /admin/organizations/:organizationId/engagements/:engagementId/requirement-sets/:requirementSetId/applicability-review` (body: `{ decision }` only, `decision` constrained server-side to the same three-value enum), both delegating to the service with sanitized `req.user`, following the existing engagement-context route conventions exactly. The review route is now scoped by the governed identity, not a proposal id, matching the service's identity-based lookup.
   20003: - `__tests__/kai-sprint2-package-2b-engagement-requirement-applicability.spec.js` (new, combined 2B-A+2B-B) - dependency-injected service-level coverage: proposal non-authoritativeness; AI/system may propose but can never review; unauthorized human and cross-tenant actors denied for both operations; `kai_standard` and non-active-governed authorities rejected at proposal and at review; exact target-identity mismatch rejected with no fuzzy inference at both steps; a review with no current decision to act on fails closed as `not_found`; the current-decision lookup is proven to be scoped by governed identity only (asserted call arguments), never a client-supplied row id; a requirement authority demoted between steps fails closed; an engagement target changed since the current decision was created fails closed instead of inheriting stale applicability; a successful review's reviewer identity/timestamp/target snapshot are all server-derived and never taken from client-supplied fields (which the input schema itself rejects as unknown, including a stray `engagementRequirementSetId`); a second review supersedes an already-confirmed decision and targets the confirmed row, not the original proposal; a reviewed `retired` decision does not qualify as current applicable in the unmodified Package 1B classifier; a writer-created `applicable` review, fed through the unmodified classifier, reaches `applicable_requirement_set_assessment_not_available`; Package 1 classifier states 1-3 (no target / no authoritative requirement set / authority not applicable) still pass; a static contract check that the generic, `engagement_id IS NULL`-only requirement-assessment repository remains untouched; a contract check that the reviewed-decision vocabulary is exactly Package 2A's three existing states.
   20004: - `__tests__/kai-sprint2-package-2b-engagement-requirement-applicability.integration.spec.js` and `scripts/kai-sprint2-package-2b-engagement-requirement-applicability-local-postgres.js` (new, combined) - runner-owned local PostgreSQL proof (own `KAI_PACKAGE_2B_ENGAGEMENT_REQUIREMENT_APPLICABILITY_DATABASE_URL`, loopback-only, synthetic data) driving the real production service/query/audit functions against the exact Package 2A schema (bootstrap + B1.1 + Package 2A migrations, unmodified): `kai_standard` rejection before any row is written; cross-tenant engagement fails closed; propose-then-review creates the reviewed/current row, writes exactly two `kai.audit_events` rows, and Package 1B reaches state 4; a replacement review supersedes the confirmed decision, the prior row is still readable by primary key with its original content intact, the Package 2A current-authority predicate resolves only to the new row, UPDATE/DELETE of the prior (superseded) row is still rejected by the unmodified append-only trigger, and a third review correctly supersedes the second row (never resurrecting or forking the first); a genuine two-connection concurrency test that opens two transactions, has both lock the same current row before either commits (proven blocked via an explicit non-resolution check), commits the first, and proves the second either finds no current row left (re-evaluated `NOT EXISTS`) or fails its insert on Package 2A's existing partial unique index (`23505`) - either way, exactly one successor row and exactly one current row exist afterward; a target changed after proposal fails closed; a cross-tenant review fails closed as `not_found`.

     197: function applicabilityRowsWithAuthority(state, { organizationId, engagementId }) {
     198:   const authority = authorityRow(state);
     199:   if (!authority) return [];
     200:   return state.applicabilityRows

     202:     .map((row) => ({

     207:       )?.engagement_requirement_set_id || null,
     208:     }));
